# fshy: Netdisk and pan, usable

> Netdisks, also called pan drives: cloud drives, many of them from China, and public AList and OpenList instances, and whether they mount.

Data as of 2026-11-26, the date of the published data. Version `12288cc5e8c1`: the first 12 hex digits of the SHA-256 of every record's Markdown, topic by topic in the one order (hosted before self-hosted, then verdict, provider name, record id); it changes whenever a record does.

This topic's parts: https://fshy.ajam.dev/t/netdisk-pan/llms-full.txt. How to read a record: https://fshy.ajam.dev/llms-full.txt.

## Allsync (managed Nextcloud, pay-as-you-go)

- `netdisk-pan/allsync` | qualifies | evidence documented | binding per-object-cap | category managed Nextcloud, paid only
- needs card yes, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-25T13:26:29.023Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-17
- url https://allsync.com/pricing | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/allsync.md
- price From EUR 4/TB/month on the 1 to 10 TB pool, excluding any tax an EU resident pays; the per-tier ladder above 1 TB is not published | parsed USD 4.51/TB-mo
- free tier: No free tier. Pay-as-you-go in two pools: a GB pool from 10 to 1,000 GB starting at EUR 0.10 per GB billed annually, and a TB pool from 1 to 10 TB starting at EUR 4 billed monthly or annually | unit: TiB | metered on: stored-bytes | read on: 2026-10-22 | source: [1] | note: EUR 4 is the TB pool's stated starting price. The page does not publish a per-tier price ladder beyond that floor, so the 10 TB figure is not established from the vendor page
- per-object cap: 'File size Unlimited' is stated as a plan property, and 'Devices Unlimited', 'Users Unlimited', 'Bandwidth Unlimited' and 'Speed Unlimited' are stated beside it. There is no published per-file ceiling | source: [1] | note: an unlimited file size claim is a claim about the absence of a limit and it is not a measured number; a Nextcloud instance still chunks uploads
- retention: Cancel any time; a refund may be requested within 14 days. No retention clock stated | source: [1]
- egress: 'Bandwidth Unlimited' as a plan property. The FAQ narrows it: 'We've set our bandwidth limits so high that 99% of our customers don't even come close', which is a fair-use statement and not an unmetered guarantee | metered: no | free allowance: unlimited, subject to a fair-use sentence | source: [1]
- identity: card | detail: Paypal, Mastercard, Visa, American Express, Ideal, Cryptocurrency, Bancontact, KBC/CBC, Belfius, SOFORT, paysafecard, CartaSi, Cartes Bancaires, SEPA Direct Debit, SEPA bank transfer
- interface: webdav, rest, rclone
- limits:
  - per-object-cap (binding): 'File size Unlimited', per the plan page, with no number behind it [1]
- binding note: Nothing is published that can bind: bandwidth, file size, users and devices are all stated as unlimited. The per-file cap is therefore unestablished rather than absent, and a plan built on it needs a chunker until one upload over 100 GB has been tried.
- tooling: rclone webdav, the Nextcloud clients for Windows, macOS, Linux, iOS and Android, and the Nextcloud AppStore. End-to-end encryption is optional and needs a master password
- composes with: Per-terabyte billing with no published egress line is the best-shaped contract of the five for a hot working set, because the cost tracks the bytes stored and not the bytes served. End-to-end encryption and server-side Office are mutually exclusive in Nextcloud, so choosing the encryption costs the collaboration features
- reliability: Registered Dutch company, EU storage. Crypto is accepted, which is unusual and which some buyers need. The 'Unlimited' claims are marketing-page assertions with no published quota table; the FAQ answers the bandwidth question with a fair-use sentence rather than a number
- notes: What is not verified is the price ladder above 1 TB: the page states a starting price and no per-tier table, so a buyer pricing 10 TB gets EUR 40/month only if the floor scales linearly, which the page does not say. That arithmetic is flagged as unconfirmed rather than published as a number.
- evidence note: The plan properties and the two pools are from the live page. The per-tier price above 1 TB is published nowhere, so `price_per_tb_month` carries the floor and the note says it is a floor. `documented`, not `measured`: nothing was ordered.
- extras:
  - access_mode: paid
  - free_tier_exists: no
  - region: Netherlands, EU storage
  - gb_pool: 10 to 1,000 GB, from EUR 0.10/GB billed annually
  - tb_pool: 1 to 10 TB, from EUR 4/TB billed monthly or annually
  - price_ladder_published: no
  - payment_methods_include_crypto: yes
  - e2ee: optional, master-password, and incompatible with server-side Office
- evidence:
  - [1] 2026-10-22: 'Users Unlimited', 'Bandwidth Unlimited', 'File size Unlimited', 'Devices Unlimited', 'Speed Unlimited' in the plan-property block
  - [1] 2026-10-22: 'Allsync GB ... Location: EU ... 10 - 1000 GB ... Free subdomain ... Starting from EUR 0,10 billed annually' and 'Allsync TB ... 1 - 10 TB ... Custom domain ... Starting from EUR 4 monthly or annually'
  - [1] 2026-10-22: FAQ: 'What is the bandwidth limit? We've set our bandwidth limits so high that 99% of our customers don't even come close'; 'If you're not satisfied, you can request a refund waiting 14 days'; payment methods list includes Cryptocurrency
  - [2] 2026-10-22: the TB product is orderable: 'Selected Product: Pay-As-You-Go - TB', a subdomain field, and a domain-or-subdomain choice, so the product page is a live order flow and not a marketing page
  - [1] 2026-10-21: the GB pool at EUR 0.10/GB billed annually and the TB pool at EUR 4, plus the same four 'Unlimited' claims
  - [1] 2026-11-19: the TB pool still starts at EUR 4 per month and the GB pool remains 10-1000 GB | capture: sha256:7060cf7eda9c7f2e846a20612bad6befbce2abaa047876e103d71adeb53230b2 | quote: Starting from €4 billed monthly or annually

## Hetzner Storage Share (managed Nextcloud)

- `netdisk-pan/hetzner-storage-share` | qualifies | evidence documented | binding per-object-cap | category managed Nextcloud, paid only
- needs card yes, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-25T13:26:30.703Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-17
- url https://www.hetzner.com/storage/storage-share/ | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/hetzner.md
- price EUR 4.29/TB/month on NX11, falling to EUR 2.74/TB/month on NX31 at 10 TB, excluding VAT, read 2026-10-22 | parsed USD 3.09/TB-mo
- free tier: No free tier, no trial, and no hourly cap. The smallest plan NX11 is 1 TB at EUR 4.29/month excluding VAT, billed by the hour to a monthly maximum | unit: TiB | metered on: stored-bytes | read on: 2026-10-22 | source: [1] | note: Hetzner writes the sizes as TB. The figure is quoted as stated and not converted
- per-object cap: Not published as a per-file cap. The limit that is published is the instance quota, and the FAQ states that an administrator's 'unlimited' per-user quota is bounded by the instance total | source: [1]
- retention: No minimum contract period. No cancellation period. | source: [1]
- egress: Storage Shares have unlimited internal and external traffic, so you'll have no problem uploading and downloading all your data to and from your Storage Share. | metered: no | free allowance: unlimited | source: [1]
- identity: card | detail: a Hetzner account with a payment method; the order form is at konsoleH. No identity document and no phone verification was seen on the order path. A new account goes through Hetzner's own risk review, which is not the same gate as a card check
- interface: webdav, rest, rclone
- limits:
  - per-object-cap (binding): this "unlimited" space will actually be limited by the overall storage quota size of the Storage Share instance that you (the admin) have bought [1]
- binding note: The instance total is bought and the traffic is unlimited, so what decides the object size is the per-file ceiling nobody publishes. A single 300 GiB file is.
- tooling: rclone webdav backend, the Nextcloud desktop and mobile clients, davfs2, curl -u, and the Nextcloud OCC command line through the managed instance
- composes with: WebDAV is the whole point: OpenList mounts it as a first-class remote, and a Cloudflare cache in front turns it into a serving origin. One 10 TB instance can also be sharded internally into per-user quotas with separate credentials, which is the only way in this survey to divide one paid unit rather than make more accounts
- reliability: A backup is performed automatically several times a day. The backup is always made to another host machine on which the instance is not running.
- notes: Managed Nextcloud with the shortest contract and a documented operations record: RAID storage with checksums, several backups a day to another host, restore through konsoleH. The tier page lists unlimited users, with 3, 7 and 10 subdomains per instance and 50, 100 and 200 simultaneous connections.
- evidence note: Every number is from the vendor's own pricing block, read. It is `documented` and not `measured` because no instance was ordered and no WebDAV endpoint was exercised: the order path needs a card.
- extras:
  - access_mode: paid
  - free_tier_exists: no
  - region: FSN1, Falkenstein, Germany
  - tier_prices_eur_month_ex_vat.NX11_1TB: 4.29
  - tier_prices_eur_month_ex_vat.NX21_5TB: 14.19
  - tier_prices_eur_month_ex_vat.NX31_10TB: 27.39
  - users_per_instance.NX11: unlimited
  - users_per_instance.NX21: unlimited
  - users_per_instance.NX31: unlimited
  - webdav: enabled on every tier; the vendor FAQ documents subdomains and CNAME for a custom domain
  - vat_position: prices are stated excluding VAT, so a private buyer in the EU pays the local rate on top

## nextfiles.one (managed Nextcloud CE and Seafile Pro)

- `netdisk-pan/nextfiles-one` | qualifies | evidence documented | binding per-object-cap | category managed Nextcloud and Seafile, paid only
- identity a placeholder: card and phone unknown | verified 2026-11-25T14:21:57.881Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-17
- url https://www.nextfiles.one/en/secure-cloud-for-private-customers-and-associations-29/ | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/nextfiles.md
- price EUR 6.99/TB/month at 1 TB rising to EUR 20.00/TB/month at 5 TB, including VAT, read 2026-11-19 | parsed USD 7.88/TB-mo
- free tier: Test for free for one month and check - cancel anytime | unit: none | metered on: stored-bytes | read on: 2026-10-22 | source: [1]
- per-object cap: Not published as a per-file cap on the private-customer plans. The plan table publishes storage, a recommended user count and the storage type ('Cluster') | source: [1]
- retention: Contract term from one month with automatic renewal for the same period; a consumer may cancel any time before a new billing period. Disaster-recovery backups of the last 7 days are kept, with managed or own backups offered for up to 10 years | days: 7 | source: [1]
- egress: No egress price and no traffic quota are published on the private-customer plans, so this field is false by the schema's own definition rather than on a stated guarantee. The features block says 'Unlimited storage space in the Cloud', which is about capacity and not about traffic | metered: no | source: [1]
- identity: card | detail: unverified: the page lists payment options but does not say the trial needs no card
- interface: webdav, rest, rclone
- limits:
  - per-object-cap (binding): not published on the private-customer plans [1]
- binding note: The price curve decides the tier and the tier is small, so the unstated per-file cap is the number a buyer cannot plan around. At EUR 20.00/TB/month on the 5 TB plan the rate is almost three times the 1 TB one, which is why it is recorded as a small-instance option.
- tooling: rclone webdav against any Nextcloud CE plan; the Nextcloud clients; administrator access to the AppStore. The Seafile Pro plans need the Seafile client or the Seafile WebDAV extension, which is a weaker tooling story
- composes with: Reseller providing managed Seafile hosting with library-level client-side encryption. The cost structure is lowest on smaller capacities and increases per terabyte on larger allocations, suiting small-scale deployments rather than bulk archives.
- reliability: Germany, ISO 27001 data centres, renewable power. The per-terabyte price rises with size, which is the opposite of every other reseller here and is the signal that the large tiers are not built for price comparison
- evidence note: The Nextcloud CE plan table was read live. `documented`: no plan was ordered and no WebDAV endpoint was exercised.
- extras:
  - access_mode: paid
  - free_tier_exists: no
  - trial: 1 month free on Nextcloud CE

  - price_includes_vat: yes

## Serverdiscounter NEXT Share (managed Nextcloud)

- `netdisk-pan/serverdiscounter-next-share` | qualifies | evidence documented | binding per-object-cap | category managed Nextcloud, paid only
- needs card yes, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-25T14:45:36.596Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-17
- url https://serverdiscounter.com/en/nextcloud | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/serverdiscounter.md
- price EUR 2.40/TB/month including VAT at 25 TB (EUR 59.95/month), rising to EUR 4.95/TB/month at 1 TB, read 2026-10-22 | parsed USD 2.7/TB-mo
- free tier: 14 Days Free Trial All features. No credit card. No commitment. | unit: TiB | metered on: stored-bytes | read on: 2026-10-22 | source: [1]
- per-object cap: 10 GB per file | bytes: 10000000000 | source: [1] | note: 10 GB is a decimal figure the vendor writes as 10 GB.
- retention: Minimum term one month, cancellation monthly, no setup fee, and a 30-day money-back guarantee | source: [1]
- egress: 'Traffic Flatrate' on all four tiers, stated as a line of the feature comparison rather than a footnote | metered: no | free allowance: flatrate | source: [1]
- identity: card | detail: a Serverdiscounter account and a card. The trial is advertised as needing no card; the paid order does. No identity document stated
- interface: webdav, rest, rclone
- limits:
  - per-object-cap (binding): 10 GB per file on every tier, including 25 TB [1]
- binding note: The 10 GB per-file ceiling is then the only number that shapes the plan: every object over 10 GB has to be chunked client-side, which is a property of the tooling and not of the quota.
- tooling: rclone webdav, the Nextcloud clients, and the admin panel for per-user quotas. 'Mount as network drive' is an advertised feature on every tier
- composes with: Full admin access plus per-user storage quotas means the archive can be divided into shares with separate credentials without buying more accounts
- reliability: Stuttgart, Germany, 100% renewable power, UPS and backup power stated. 30-day money-back. The per-file cap is the friction and it is uniform across tiers, so it never improves with size
- notes: The trial is 14 days and needs no card, which is the cheapest way to test a WebDAV mount before paying.
- evidence note: Prices and the per-file cap are from the vendor's own comparison table, read. `documented`, not `measured`: no order was placed, so the WebDAV endpoint and the real per-file behaviour are unexercised.
- extras:
  - access_mode: paid
  - free_tier_exists: no
  - trial: 14 days, no card, deleted unless extended
  - region: Stuttgart, Germany
  - contract: 1 month minimum, monthly cancellation, no setup fee

- composes with: Terms section 3.1 explicitly routes anyone needing more than 5 TB to a bespoke cold-storage plan, so it is a 1 TB building block and not a pool. Its value is the WebDAV endpoint, which mounts into TrueNAS Cloud Sync, rclone, Duplicati and restic without a wrapper
- reliability: Zero durability commitment in the terms plus an explicit rule against the several-accounts pattern that would make it useful at 20 TB. The domestic storage node carries a compliance clause, so the overseas node is the only one that fits
- notes: The service provides no formal uptime or data durability SLA.

- identity: email | detail: an email address at one of a fixed list of domains (including gmail.com, qq.com, icloud.com, 163.com and yahoo.com) and an emailed six-digit code; the register form has username, password, confirm, email, email_code and an agreement checkbox with no phone or mobile input present

- limits:
  - per-ip-shaping (binding): 1 TB of bandwidth per month on the free tier [1]
  - write-gated: a check-in grants 5 GB (checkin_reward_gb is 5), and the site config sets the check-in storage limit to 505 GB [2]
- binding note: 1 TB a month corresponds to 33 GB a day, meaning the transfer allowance binds before the 500 GB storage ceiling does under sustained extraction.
- tooling: rclone s3 against s3.1yunpan.com, rclone webdav, s3cmd, any S3 SDK, and a raw GET on the per-user host. Registration is an email address and an emailed six-digit code with no phone field in the DOM; Google and GitHub OAuth are also offered
- composes with: The strongest chaining candidate in its group: a free bucket that answers both S3 and WebDAV drops into a chunk-and-replicate scheme as a first-class target with no AList shim. The 1 TB monthly bandwidth is the binding number, not the 500 GB of space
- reliability: The site config sets upload.temporarily_disabled to false, so file upload is enabled.
- notes: 500 GB reachable on an email address with no phone is unusual, and the S3 endpoint answering real S3 error semantics is what makes it useful: rclone, restic and Duplicati can each treat an account as a bucket. The check-in ladder is a daily quota rather than a wall-clock trial, so it is repeatable, but it also means the account is worth less the moment the streak stops.

## Amazon Photos (Prime)

- `netdisk-pan/amazon-photos` | qualifies-with-caveat | evidence reported | binding write-gated | category retail-ecosystem photo cloud
- needs card no, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-26T02:15:13.860Z (inherited) | stale on 2026-12-11
- url https://www.amazon.com/gp/help/customer/display.html?nodeId=G6PT8TMLM9NVZCSL | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/amazon.md
- price Bundled with Prime at about USD 139/yr; the unlimited photo tier has no standalone price
- free tier: 5 GB for every Amazon customer. Unlimited full-resolution photo storage plus 5 GB of video for Amazon Prime members | unit: GB | metered on: stored-bytes | read on: 2026-10-21 | source: [1]
- per-object cap: 5 GB of video even on Prime; photos are unlimited. Paid plans exist above 5 GB | source: [1]
- retention: Files are deleted after 2 years (730 days) of inactivity, with an email notice at 18 months (547 days) | days: 730 | source: [1]
- egress: No download fee is stated; an over-quota account can still view, download, and share what it already stored | metered: no | source: [1]
- identity: email | detail: an Amazon account and a Prime membership. Prime is the price of admission, so unlimited is bundled and not free
- interface: rest, cli
- limits:
  - per-object-cap: 5 GB of video total, even on Prime; photos are unlimited but must be photographs [1]
  - write-gated (binding): cookie authentication with no OAuth or key flow, so the credential is a browser session that expires [2]
- binding note: The capacity is genuinely unlimited for photographs and the gate is the credential: a browser cookie with no OAuth flow, which expires and cannot be renewed programmatically. That makes it a manual-ingest target at any scale.
- tooling: unofficial and cookie-authenticated only: trevorhobenshield/amazon_photos in Python, entourloop/amazon-photo-uploader in Rust, and an MCP server. There is no OAuth and no API key, and every wrapper says cookies expire and must be refreshed by hand. No rclone backend
- composes with: The classic unlimited-photos shape: if arbitrary image bytes are accepted, and they are because it is a photo service, then a photo-shaped payload is free across accounts. The 5 GB video cap is what stops it being a general drive, and the absence of an API stops it being a pipeline
- reliability: Cookie-authenticated wrappers break on any change to Amazon's login flow; the MCP wrapper's own README calls it an unofficial API that may break. Photo-only means ingest may re-encode or reject non-image payloads
- notes: Combines evaluation of both the photo storage and video limits. Unlimited full-resolution photo backup is available to Amazon Prime members, while non-photo video storage is capped at 5 GB. Authentication requires web session cookies.
- extras:
  - access_mode: paid
  - free_tier_exists: yes
  - api: unofficial cookie auth only
  - aliases: Amazon Photos (Prime)
  - cross_topic_record: storage/amazon-photos
  - cross_topic_note: A record for this provider already exists under the storage topic; this one is scoped to its role as a pan, following the same split netdisk-pan/lanzou uses.
- evidence:
  - [1] 2026-10-21: '5 GB of free storage for all Amazon customers. Unlimited full-resolution photo storage, plus 5 GB of video storage, for Amazon Prime members'
  - [3] 2026-10-21: 'unlimited photo storage and 5 GB of video storage to Amazon Prime members in eight marketplaces'
  - [4] 2026-10-21: a Python client with md5 deduplication that preserves directory structure on upload
  - [2] 2026-10-21: 'Amazon Photos has no OAuth or API key flow'; 'Cookie auth is fragile'

## crt.sh certificate transparency search (AList and OpenList hostnames)

- `netdisk-pan/crt-sh-alist-openlist-ct-channel` | qualifies-with-caveat | evidence measured | binding request-rate | category index-list
- needs card no, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-25T13:26:29.022Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-17
- url https://crt.sh/ | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/crt.md
- free tier: unverified: the captured page is only a certificate search form and states no allowance, quota or price | unit: none | metered on: none | read on: 2026-11-03 | source: [1]
- per-object cap: not applicable; it is a search over issued certificates | source: [1]
- retention: a certificate stays listed after it expires; exclude=expired limits a query to certificates that are still valid | source: [1]
- egress: no egress; it is a search answer, and one prefix query returned a JSON body of several hundred KB | metered: no | source: [1]
- identity: anonymous | detail: an open search page and JSON endpoint, read with no account
- interface: rest
- limits:
  - request-rate (binding): a single wildcard query alist.%25 returned a truncated result set (the four known hosts alist.qsesvick.top, alist.tianmoy.cn, alist.shenzjd.com and alist.jiejingfan.vip were all missing from it) and crt.sh answered 502, 404 or no reply on many attempts; one query per next character, retried, was needed [2]
- binding note: crt.sh throttles and truncates wide wildcard queries, so a prefix partition is required and the result is a lower bound.
- tooling: GET https://crt.sh/?q=alist.PREFIX%25&output=json&exclude=expired, one query per next character; a read client needs no login. The hostnames then go through GET /api/public/settings and GET /api/me on each host; the listing step is a POST that a GET-only probe cannot make
- composes with: a hostname list for the capability probe in the public-instances protocol, to be run after the hosted index lists (bangumi.tv, kuhehe.github.io) and in addition to them
- reliability: crt.sh answered HTTP 502, 404 or no reply on a share of its requests and needed repeated tries; a hostname in CT is a certificate, not a live instance.
- notes: Certificate transparency lists hosts whose name starts with alist or openlist, including hosts that no forum or GitHub index names. It holds 7447 distinct hostnames with such a label (wildcard form included), 4 with an existing record. Of these, 2356 return the AList or OpenList envelope on GET /api/public/settings and 588 an enabled guest session on GET /api/me, so 586 new hostnames pass steps 0 and 1. The listing step (POST /api/fs/list) is not done, so none counts as a readable instance. Hosts named pan, cloud or share are not reachable by this channel.
- extras:
  - access_mode: index
  - ct_hostnames: 7447
  - with_existing_record: 4
  - answered_settings: 2356
  - guest_enabled: 588
  - new_guest_enabled: 586
  - listing_step_run: no

## Ctfile (城通网盘, ctfile.com)

- `netdisk-pan/ctfile` | qualifies-with-caveat | evidence documented | binding retention-clamped | category consumer-pan
- needs card no, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-25T14:05:48.227Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-17
- url https://www.ctfile.com/ | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/ctfile.md
- free tier: 5 TB of shared space on the free tier, 20 GB per file, and no upload speed cap | unit: TB | metered on: stored-bytes | read on: 2026-10-22 | source: [1] | note: the landing page and the register page agree on 5 TB and 20 GB; the help centre states the same 20 GB per file
- per-object cap: Free users: Max 20GB per file | bytes: 20000000000 | source: [2] | note: terms section 3.2.8 states the same 20 GB as a default
- retention: files are deleted after 30 consecutive days without a sign-in on a free account, or 365 days on a paid account | days: 30 | source: [3] | note: terms section 3.2.5, quoted; the landing page states it as 'log in once a month to renew'
- egress: downloads are metered by tier rather than by byte: free accounts have no published volume cap but a slow channel, Standard is stated at 'up to 5MB/sec', and Super removes the limit; no per-GB egress price is published | metered: no | source: [4]
- identity: email | detail: email, password, captcha
- interface: rest, webdav, cli
- limits:
  - retention-clamped (binding): 30 consecutive days without a sign-in deletes files on a free account [3]
  - per-object-cap: 20 GB per file on the free tier [3]
  - request-rate: 200,000 files and 10,000 folders per account; 10,000 file uploads per day; 5 concurrent download and 5 concurrent upload threads [3]
- binding note: The identity gate is the one that usually closes a Chinese pan, and this one is open; what binds is the 30-day inactivity clock, which makes it a working set and not an archive.
- tooling: first-party desktop client and a WebDAV endpoint; the free tier advertises WebDAV and the Super tier advertises 'WebDAV batch download'; the nfd-parser project ships a ct driver
- composes with: the only Chinese pan in this survey that a foreigner can self-register for with an email address alone, so it is the one that can sit under a self-hosted bridge without a +86 number
- reliability: Over-quota triggers deletion of the excess without prior notice, and the inactivity clock deletes a free account's files at 30 days; a monthly sign-in cron keeps it alive and nothing else does.
- notes: 5 TB free, 20 GB per file, email-only registration, WebDAV advertised. The 30-day inactivity deletion is the number that decides how it can be used: a monthly cron job keeps it, and nothing else does. Terms section 3.2.7 also caps the account at 200,000 files, which at 20 GB per file is a 4 PB nominal ceiling and therefore not the constraint.
- extras:
  - access_mode: registration-gated
  - registration: 请输入图片中的5位字母和数字 (5-character image verification code)
  - webdav_tier: advertised on the free tier and on Super as batch download
  - inactivity_days_free: 30
  - inactivity_days_paid: 365
  - file_count_cap: 200000
  - daily_upload_cap: 10000

- limits:
  - write-gated (binding): unlimited applies only to content captured with a GoPro camera, plus media trimmed from it in the Quik app; GoPro footage edited with non-GoPro software and re-imported counts as non-GoPro [3]
  - per-object-cap: non-GoPro storage is limited to MP4, Quicktime/MOV, JPEG, ASF and PNG files [3]
- binding note: The capacity is unlimited on paper and the gate is a provenance rule plus an upload path nobody has documented. Until one large non-GoPro file is uploaded and accepted, the offer is 100 GB at USD 59.99/yr, which is bad.
- tooling: reverse-engineered and read-only in every public wrapper: himewel/gopro-api in Python, dustin/gopro in Go with 139 stars and a push on 2026-10-13, gsora/gpdwn, mvisonneau/gpcd. All of them search, download and sync; none uploads. Auth is a bearer token lifted from a plus.gopro.com web session
- composes with: The most valuable unresolved lead in its group and it is one upload experiment, not a research question. The terms draw the line at provenance by device, not by file inspection, so if ingest does not verify capture provenance then unlimited capacity at USD 59.99/yr exists; if it does, the offer collapses to 100 or 500 GB
- reliability: No public wrapper implements upload, so the write path is undocumented rather than absent: the Quik app clearly uploads. Treat the write path as unproven. The supported non-GoPro file types are MP4, Quicktime/MOV, JPEG, ASF and PNG
- notes: Testing evaluates whether non-GoPro media qualifies for unlimited backup or remains restricted to the 100 GB cap. Pricing for general non-GoPro storage runs approximately USD 120 per TB per year, making device provenance verification the defining constraint.
- extras:
  - access_mode: paid
  - upload_api: none documented; the Quik app uploads but no wrapper implements it
  - provenance_rule: by capture device, excluding Fusion
  - test_that_settles_it: one account, one large non-GoPro upload, then read the quota back through api.gopro.com

## kuhehe.github.io AList site index (the 2024 cohort source)

- `netdisk-pan/kuhehe-alist-index` | qualifies-with-caveat | evidence measured | binding retention-clamped | category index-list
- needs card no, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-25T13:26:29.021Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-17

- free tier: not stated: https://kuhehe.github.io/20240120224502/ is a static HTML post listing AList sites and prints no allowance, quota or price | unit: none | metered on: none | read on: 2026-10-30 | source: [1]
- per-object cap: not applicable; it is a list, not a service | source: [1]
- retention: unverified: the page is still served; the dated sections were not compared for which is newest | source: [2]
- egress: no egress; it is HTML | metered: no | source: [1]
- identity: anonymous | detail: an open HTML page
- interface: rest
- limits:
  - retention-clamped (binding): the page is edited in place rather than republished, so its permalink date is not its content date [2]
- binding note: The bias that matters is not the page dying but the page changing: hosts were added to a permalink after its date, so a survival rate computed from the URL's date overstates survival.

## Lark / 飞书 Drive via multi-tenant app credentials (the FeiSync pattern)

- `netdisk-pan/lark-drive-via-multi-tenant-app-credentials` | qualifies-with-caveat | evidence reported | binding per-object-cap | category platform storage reached through app credentials
- needs card no, phone yes, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-26T02:15:14.532Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-11
- url https://open.larksuite.com/app | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/larksuite.md
- free tier: 100 GB per Lark tenant. '任意扩容，只要添加 App ID 和 App Secret 就可以免费获得100G空间，单文件250M，简单说就是容量上限取决于你的号数量' | unit: GiB | metered on: stored-bytes | read on: 2026-10-21 | source: [1]
- per-object cap: 250 MB per file | bytes: 250000000 | source: [1]
- retention: While the tenant persists. No retention clock published | source: [2]
- egress: Unmetered in practice; there is no egress line item on the free tier | metered: no | source: [1]
- identity: email | detail: a distinct email address or phone number per tenant. Lark has historically required an overseas IP to register outside the mainland, which is a feature here
- interface: rest
- limits:
  - per-object-cap (binding): 250 MB per file [1]
  - request-rate: apps inside one tenant share that tenant's 100 GB, so a second app is not a second quota [1]
- tooling: FeiSync v1.0.0 by fish2018, Tauri and Rust with a Vue front end, presented on linux.do on 2025-12-28: multi-tenant routing, one-way or two-way cron sync, resumable transfer and an open HTTP API. The underlying API is the Lark Drive open platform v1 at /drive/v1/files
- composes with: This is the Google service-account pattern reimplemented for Lark: create N free tenants, register a custom app in each, and FeiSync load-balances writes across them for 100 GB times N in one flat namespace. 250 MB per file is the binding number, not the bytes
- reliability: Two constraints bite: 250 MB per file caps the cold-archive case hard, and app-permission changes break the wrapper. Tenant creation needs a distinct identity each time
- notes: The most directly applicable of the app-credential patterns, because tenancy is free and self-service. The 250 MB file cap is what makes it a shard target rather than a store, and that is a job for a chunker rather than a blocker.
- extras:
  - access_mode: registration-gated
  - wrapper: FeiSync v1.0.0 (fish2018)
  - quota_per_tenant_bytes_derived: 107374182400
  - per_file_cap_bytes_derived: 250000000
  - overseas_ip_required: yes
  - aliases: Lark / 飞书 Drive via multi-tenant app credentials (FeiSync pattern)
- evidence:
  - [1] 2026-10-21: '免费无限扩容，每个账号免费扩容100G，单文件250M' and '不能是同一个企业的不同应用，会共享100G'
  - [3] 2026-10-21: 'larksuite所谓飞书海外版，必须要海外IP才能注册'; '海外ip，会成功'
  - [4] 2026-10-21: 'Lark 免费用户就有自定域名邮箱、100 GB 存储'
  - [1] 2026-11-19: the FeiSync thread still states 100G free per app tenant and a 250M single-file cap | capture: sha256:d05277780c2413ece5e27ae3e244de4e106e401395ee005dda2eaad8296701a6 | quote: 任意扩容，只要添加`App ID`和`App Secret`就可以免费获得100G空间网盘，单文件250M
  - [4] 2026-11-19: free Lark tenants are obtained with an email address through web registration | capture: sha256:c9c40b5e70ea9246aa64639fb53936d411b26858ca22c07ba41a58b169850531 | quote: 目前 Lark 免费用户就有自定域名邮箱、100 GB 存储且支持 SMTP 和 IMAP。可网页注册

## lz.qaiu.top, the keyless multi-pan direct-link resolver

- `netdisk-pan/lz-qaiu-top-netdisk-fast-download-keyless-multi-pan-dire` | qualifies-with-caveat | evidence measured | binding write-gated | category resolver, not a store
- needs card no, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-25T15:00:46.533Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-17
- url https://lz.qaiu.top | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/qaiu.md
- free tier: free public resolver, no allowance stated: https://github.com/qaiu/netdisk-fast-download/blob/main/README.md lists lz.qaiu.top as a 公益 (free public) instance and prints no quota, unit or price for it | unit: none | metered on: none | read on: 2026-10-30 | source: [1]
- per-object cap: Not applicable; it stores nothing | source: [2]
- retention: Not applicable. The instance caches a resolved ecpan link for 5 minutes, and the signed redirect carries X-Amz-Expires=86400, so a URL is resolved per session rather than stored | days: 1 | source: [2]
- egress: Free. It issues a 302 to the real CDN origin, so the bytes never traverse the resolver: verified against China Mobile EOS with X-Amz-Expires=86400 | metered: no | source: [2]
- identity: anonymous | detail: no account, no key and no cookie for the free drivers. The professional endpoint needs an X-API-Key
- interface: rest
- limits:
  - write-gated (binding): it resolves and does not store: there is no write path at all [2]
  - request-rate: no per-client limit is published for the public instance; the owner announces that from August 2026 requests with no Referer header may be rate limited, with no figure; 100 parallel requests with no Referer from one address were all answered 200 [3]
  - per-ip-shaping: feijipan blocks mainland datacentre IPs [2]
- binding note: The resolver is free, keyless and fast, and it stores nothing, so what binds is that it only resolves: there is no write path, and the pans most in demand are behind the keyed endpoint.
- tooling: two public keyless forms, GET https://lz.qaiu.top/parser?url=<enc>&pwd=<pin> which 302s, or the short form https://lz.qaiu.top/ec/<key>@<pin>. A mirror at lz0.qaiu.top. Self-hostable from the repository with Docker on ports 6400 and 6401
- composes with: Pair with a public AList that lists share links, or with any resource site, to fetch Chinese-pan content without an account. The server software can also be installed and run on a rented or free machine, the last resort after the hosted instances
- reliability: Alive and actively maintained, Trendshift-listed. It is the project's official free public instance. The README asks users not to over-rely on it. The README says heavy request volume can get the server limited by the pan vendors.
- notes: The single highest-leverage piece of glue in the topic and the reason the pan survey is usable at all. Two endpoints, and the split is the finding: the free one covers lanzou, ilanzou, ctfile, wenshushu, feijipan and ecpan, and the keyed one adds Baidu, Xunlei, Quark, UC, 139, 189 and Unicom.
- self hosted: no
- extras:
  - access_mode: anonymous
  - self_hostable: yes
  - professional_endpoint: https://189.qaiu.top
  - mirror: lz0.qaiu.top
  - free_drivers: lz; iz; ec; fj; fc; ye; ws; le; qqw; ct; ce
  - keyed_drivers: qk; uc; p139; pwo; p189
  - aliases: lz.qaiu.top / netdisk-fast-download — keyless multi-pan direct-link resolver (30+ Chinese pans)
  - sibling_record: netdisk-pan/netdisk-fast-download-anonymous-multi-pan-parser covers the project as a self-hostable parser; this record covers the hosted instance and what it resolves

  - probe:China Mobile EOS redirect target, Range 0-524287 2026-10-22: 206 with 524,288 bytes, the file identified as ISO Media MP4, Server ChinaMobileEOS, so the redirect delivered real bytes

## netdisk-fast-download (lz.qaiu.top / 189.qaiu.top)

- `netdisk-pan/netdisk-fast-download` | qualifies-with-caveat | evidence measured | binding per-object-cap | category direct-link-resolver
- needs card no, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-25T14:05:51.157Z (live) | stale on 2026-12-17
- url https://github.com/qaiu/netdisk-fast-download | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/github-qaiu.md
- free tier: the public lz.qaiu.top endpoint parses share links for the drivers it lists at no charge and with no key | unit: unlimited | metered on: request-count | read on: 2026-10-22 | source: [1] | note: the free endpoint is explicitly not to be depended on; the README asks users to self-host
- per-object cap: the resolved file's own size; the resolver imposes none, and the driver's limit applies (100 MiB on classic Lanzou, 100 GB on 123pan above which a login is needed) | source: [2]
- retention: the resolved URL is short-lived and must be re-minted per request | source: [2]
- egress: the resolver returns a 302 to the pan's own CDN, so the bytes come from the pan and not from the resolver | metered: no | source: [1]
- identity: anonymous | detail: the free endpoint needs no key; the Professional endpoint requires an X-API-Key header or a token parameter
- interface: rest
- limits:
  - request-rate: no published limit on lz.qaiu.top, and the README warns that heavy use gets the server restricted by the pan vendors [1]
  - per-object-cap (binding): 123pan files above 100 MiB now require a login; classic Lanzou is capped at 100 MiB [2]
- binding note: The resolver itself is unlimited and free; what limits it is the driver table, where the pans worth resolving have the smallest per-file caps or now demand a login.
- tooling: GET /parser?url=...&pwd=... returns a 302 to the direct link; the short form /<driver>/<shareKey>@<pwd> does the same; the project is Java and self-hostable
- composes with: the read path for pans that have no WebDAV and no mountable API: resolve the share link to a direct URL, then fetch it with a plain ranged GET
- reliability: The README warns that lz.qaiu.top should not be depended on, that heavy request volume gets the server restricted by the pan vendors, and that FeijiPan blocks most mainland datacentre IPs and bans accounts frequently.
- notes: The free tier and the Professional tier split exactly along the pans that are hard: Lanzou, iLanzou, Ctfile, FeijiPan, ecpan, Fangcloud and 123pan resolve without a key, while Baidu, Xunlei, Quark, UC, 139 and 189 sit behind the key and return 401. That split is the map of which pans still resist programmatic access.

## netdisk-fast-download (NFD), the anonymous multi-pan parser

- `netdisk-pan/netdisk-fast-download-anonymous-multi-pan-parser` | qualifies-with-caveat | evidence reported | binding write-gated | category resolver, not a store
- needs card no, phone no, ID no, institution no | verified 2026-11-26T02:15:15.157Z (inherited) | stale on 2026-12-11
- url https://github.com/qaiu/netdisk-fast-download | page https://fshy.ajam.dev/p/github-qaiu.md
- free tier: free software with a free public instance, no allowance stated: https://github.com/qaiu/netdisk-fast-download links a 公益 (free public) instance at lz.qaiu.top and prints no quota, unit or price | unit: none | metered on: none | read on: 2026-10-30 | source: [1]
- per-object cap: Not applicable, it stores nothing. What it resolves inherits the upstream per-file cap | source: [1]
- retention: Not applicable; the server caches a resolved link for a per-pan time in minutes set in its configuration (shipped values: 5 for ecpan, 20 for feijipan, 30 for lanzou), and a pan with no entry is not cached | source: [1]
- egress: Free. It issues a 302 to the real CDN origin, so the bytes never traverse the resolver and you download straight from the pan's own CDN | metered: no | source: [1]
- identity: anonymous | detail: the hosted instance is keyless. Self-hosting needs a machine the user runs and no account with any pan; individual pan downloads may still need the user's own Quark/UC cookie or 123 Authorization
- interface: rest
- limits:
  - request-rate: the server code has an optional per-IP request limiter, off unless rateLimit.enable is set; the example in the shipped configuration is 10 requests per 10 seconds on /v2/ paths [2]
  - write-gated (binding): Quark at or below 10MiB resolves without login and larger files need a valid SVIP/VIP account cookie or a downloadable H5 address; UC cannot be anonymous even for small files and still needs a cookie; feijipan and ilanzou need auth above 100 MB; 123 downloads usually need the user's own Authorization in the local configuration [1]
- binding note: The resolver is free and stores nothing, so the auth table binds it: Quark is anonymous only up to 10MiB, UC never is, feijipan and ilanzou need auth above 100 MB, and 123 usually needs the user's own Authorization. Baidu and Xunlei moved to the paid professional endpoint; Quark and UC did not.
- tooling: hosted instances first: GET https://lz.qaiu.top/parser?url=<encoded share>&pwd=<code> returns a 302 to the pan's CDN, also on the mirror lz0.qaiu.top. Last resort: the user installs and runs the open-source server (a release archive on Java 17 or later, or the Docker image) on a rented or free machine, with the API on port 6400 and the web tool on port 6401; JavaScript and Java parser plugins extend it
- composes with: Resolve a share link with a hosted instance first (lz.qaiu.top, mirror lz0.qaiu.top), then fetch the returned direct URL with a plain ranged GET. A server the user installs and runs is the last resort; a pan that blocks datacentre addresses, such as feijipan for mainland ranges, is reached through a proxy set in its configuration
- reliability: Individual parsers break whenever a provider changes its markup. The project is actively maintained, so pinning a version and watching the README changelog is the operational answer. The README warns that feijipan blocks mainland datacentre IPs and that the public instance should not be depended on
- notes: Not storage: it is the fetch path for other people's storage. The endpoint split changed: the free public instance lz.qaiu.top now also covers Quark and UC through the open-source parser, while the paid professional endpoint adds Baidu, Xunlei, Guangya, China Mobile, Unicom and Tianyi. The program can also be self-hosted by the user on a rented or free machine; the hosted lz.qaiu.top instance is the last-resort option only when no hosted parser exists, and here it is the primary free path.
- self hosted: no
- extras:
  - access_mode: anonymous
  - free_drivers: lz; iz; ec; fj; fc; ye; ws; le; qqw; ct; ce
  - keyed_drivers: qk; uc; p139; pwo; p189
  - professional_endpoint: https://189.qaiu.top
  - self_hostable: yes
  - aliases: netdisk-fast-download (NFD) — anonymous multi-pan parser

- free tier: 2 TB free at registration: '注册即享 2T 空间，会员畅享 500T 空间'. 100 GB per file free and 1 TB for members | unit: TB | metered on: stored-bytes | read on: 2026-10-21 | source: [1]

  - per-object-cap: 100 GB per file on the free tier [5]
  - request-rate: 3 free offline or magnet cloud-adds a day [4]
- binding note: 2 TB of capacity is the largest free allocation in this survey after Ctfile, and 2 GB a day at high speed is. The traffic cap is what decides the plan, and as a cold archive it never binds.
- tooling: a merged OpenList driver, guangyapan, which gives WebDAV and 302 direct links once mounted; two-stage SMS login needing a client id, an X-Captcha-Token and an X-Device-Sign; CloudDrive2 v1.0.11 or later reads it
- composes with: Provides 2 TB of free storage per account with OpenList driver integration, WebDAV support, and TV streaming clients. For active content distribution, the 2 GB daily bandwidth limit is prohibitive. For cold archival storage, the daily limit does not impede initial single-pass uploads.
- reliability: Auth is SMS-only, so an account with only an email address cannot register. The OpenList docs carry an explicit danger block: use for multi-person sharing, image hosting, software hosting or video external links causes the provider to close accounts, which is the exact workload this survey targets. Xunlei's two previous netdisks both died, and the price rose from CNY 108 to 188 within months of launch
- notes: The two numbers the marketing merges are capacity and traffic, and they are ten thousand times apart in practical effect. The SMS-only registration is what closes it.
